2. TTCS’ Organisation and Experience  
 
2.1 Organisation 
 
Twenty Third Century Systems (TTCS) is a Pan African business solutions provider, incorporated in 1996. 
TTCS has focused on implementing SAP based solutions across Africa and the Middle East in sectors such 
as Government, Mining, Manufacturing, Social Secu rity as well as Tax and Revenue collecting agencies. 
TTCS has a diverse pool of consultants who are both functional and technical experts in the scope of 
various offerings. These include SAP’s various modules, such as Financials, Logistics, Human Capital 
Management, Analytics, Mobility, Cloud and Database Technologies. TTCS also offers SAP on the cloud 
for various market segments such as Small to Medium Enterprises (SMEs). We successfully achieve this 
using up-to-date SAP approved methodologies for timely and effective product delivery and support.  
 
TTCS is present across Africa and in particular has offices in Zimbabwe, Zambia, Malawi, South Africa, 
Kenya, Uganda, Rwanda and Nigeria. The driving philosophy is to collaborate with local partners to build 
local skills that then become part of the TTCS global skills pool and are able to provide a local in -country 
delivery and support capability.  
 
TTCS has built a reputation of superior responsiveness to customer’s diverse needs; the goal being that of 
providing solutions that are relevant to customers’ businesses. TTCS takes a customer -centric approach to 
all issue solving, while striving to continually enhance customer experience to satisfaction. Repeat and 
long-standing clients are testimony of TTCS’ commitment to excellence. TTCS, through its Customer 
Services Division was one of the companies to be certified by SAP as a Partner Centre of Expertise (PCoE) 
through which online support is provided to clients on a 24/7 basis. 
 
TTCS is the largest SAP Ser vice Partner in Sub -Saharan Africa excluding the Sout h African market and 
has over 19 years SAP experience. 
 
TTCS SAP Partnership Status 
TTCS is an SAP Gold Partner, an accolade awarded for excellent and diligent workmanship on SAP 
implementations. SAP Gold Partners are recognised by the programme as experts who deliver 
transformational, highly functional SAP solutions across the entire customer engagement lifecycle - through 
sales, implementation services and support, system design and operational suppo rt for their customers. 
 
TTCS Method 
 Extensive SAP Knowledge 
 Business Knowledge 
 Insight 
 Focus on Delivery 
 Best Practices 
 Active Customer Support 
 
TTCS Strategy 
TTCS’ strategy is to continuously build on its relationship with SAP and be the preferred partne r of choice in 
the deployment of SAP solutions across the chosen key market segments. The ultimate goal is to help 
TTCS clients achieve global competitiveness through business transformation using technology and 
thereby accelerate development initiatives in Africa to both private and public entities.  
 
From the knowledge and experience gained from different assignments on the African continent, TTCS has 
developed a keen awareness of how to successfully accomplish the organisational objectives of its 
customers.

Notable Accolades 
Partner Centre of Expertise (PCoE) 
To ensure that all indirect customers receive the same high quality support, SAP introduced a dedicated 
PCOE certification, which is exclusively available for SAP PartnerEdge Channel Partners providing support 
for SAP Business All-In-One and SAP Business Objects solutions. TTCS is one of the few certified partners 
in Africa.

About SAP 
Headquartered in Walldorf, Germany, with locations in more than 130 countries, SAP AG is the world 
leader in enterprise software and software-related services. Based on market capitalisation, it is the world’s 
third largest independent software manufacturer with 43 years’ experience in developing enterprise 
software.  
 
As the market leader in enterprise application software, SAP helps companies of all sizes and industries 
run better. From back office to boardroom, warehouse to storefront, desktop to mobile device – SAP 
empowers people and organisations to work together more efficiently and use business insight more 
effectively to stay ahead of the competition. SAP applications and services enable more 
than 253,500 customers to operate profitably, adapt continuously, and grow sustainably.

4. IMIS User Requirements   
 
4.1 Solution Overview 
The SAP ERP application is a world -class, fully integrated solution that fulfills the core business needs of 
midsize and large organizations across all industries and market sectors. Powered by the SAP 
NetWeaver and HANA technology platforms, SAP helps ent erprises perform financials, human capital 
management, procurement and logistics, product development and manufacturing, and sales and service, 
supported by functionality for analytics, corporate services, and end-user service delivery.  
 
SAP has invested years of research in water utilities industry . Below the table is the SAP value map in 
which individual components have been selected to address specific requirements for NOCMA  
 
The following table shows the key composition of the SAP ERP solution which are we are  proposing to 
NOCMA.

The following solution map shows the proposed SAP modules for NOCMA. These have been mapped to 
NOCMA’s business process requirements as defined in the RFP. The solution map below depicts the 
solution components we are proposing in order to effectively meet all NOCMA’s requirements.  
 
 
 
The areas of business covered are: 
 
SAP ERP Reporting 
SAP ERP is a complete, integrated solution for reporting and business i ntelligence, including financial and 
operational reporting. SAP will enable NOCMA to clearly understand profit drivers for the business, tightly 
link strategic plans to operational performance, and support a single analytical platform for their 
enterprise.  
  
Key functions include the following: 
  
 Financial Reporting 
SAP ERP provides financial reporting that will help NOCMA define financial targets, develop a realistic 
business plan, and monitor costs and revenue during execution. Comprehensive financial re porting 
enables businesses to create and simulate value flows in a cross -functional, cross -company, or 
collaborative environment. SAP ERP also supports corporate performance management by providing 
strategy management, performance monitoring, and business consolidation functionality.

i. Financial Accounting 
The main objective of Financial Accounting is to provide an overall view of external accounting as  
required by various accounting principles (IAS, US -GAAP, local GAAP). Collecting and storing all 
business events from different business systems in an integrated system ensures that account 
management is complete and synchronized at all times. 
 
 General Ledger (G/L) 
Provides a comprehensive picture for external accounting and accounts. Records all business 
transactions (primary postings as well as settlements from internal accounting) in a software 
system that is fully integrated with  all the other operational areas of a company to ensure that the 
accounting data is always complete and accurate. 
 
 Accounts Receivable 
Records and manages customer accounting data via the Accounts Receivable (AR) component. 
Connects AR directly to the G/L using a special reconciliation account. 
 
 Accounts Payable 
Records and manages vendor accounting data via the Accounts Payable (AP) component. 
Connects AP directly to the G/L using a special reconciliation account. 


=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 21 of 275  
 
 Contract Accounting 
Covers all standard accounts receivable and accounts payable functions and provides new 
functions with a high degree of flexibility. Manages receivables and payables in a sub -ledger that 
follows t he aspects of mass data processing and system integration. Contract Accounting 
represents a solution that is most valuable for customers with a large volume of business partners 
and documents to process. 
 
 Fixed Assets Accounting 
Maintains and analyzes fixed assets according to generally accepted rules in the company's 
country. Delivers country templates with the system. 
  
 Bank Accounting 
Includes the management of bank maste r data, cash balance management (check and bill of 
exchange management), and the creation and processing of incoming and outgoing payments.  
 
 Cash Journal Accounting 
Manages cash transactions via Cash Journal Accounting. Calculates and displays the opening and 
closing balances and the receipts and payments totals automatically.  
 
 Inventory Accounting 
Supports the valuation and reporting of inventory according to different regulations.  Carries 
inventory values in two additional currencies/valuations via the Material Ledger component. 
Performs all goods movements in the Material Ledger in up to three currencies or valuations. 
Translates currency amounts into foreign currencies at histori cal exchange rates at the time of 
posting. Supports the determination of actual costs (compared to standard costs) for externally 
procured materials and materials produced in -house via Actual Costing. Valuates material 
inventories, such as raw materials as well as semi-finished and finished products, accordingly. 
 
 Tax Accounting 
Supports the calculation and reporting of taxes on sales and purchases and withholding tax.  
 
 Accrual Accounting 
Manages accounts pa yables and receivables differently than the required periodic assignment of 
costs and revenues. Records revenues when they are earned, not when the receivables are 
disclosed; costs according to the consumption of material or services, not when the obligati on 
arises, via Accrual Accounting. 
 
 Local Close 
Supports speed and transparency of the closing process by means of application integration, 
process automation, reconciliation and collaboration functionality. 
 
 Financial Statements 
As part of the period or year -end closing, individual companies create financial statements 
according to country-specific regulations. 
  
ii. Management Accounting 
Management accounting provides valuation and  recording of financial data as the basis for all cost - 
and revenue-related reporting. 
 
 Profit Center Accounting 
Assigns costs, revenues and balance sheet items to internal areas of responsibility to provide the 
basis for e xternal segment reporting and internal reporting by profit center. Supports both the 
period accounting and the cost -of-sales approach. Can be implemented either as an extension of 
the general ledger to include the dimensions segment and profit center (new general ledger) or as 
a separate ledger specifically for Profit Center Accounting (classic general ledger).

 Cost Center and Internal Order Accounting 
Records costs incurred during the company operations by assigning them to cost centers 
(assigned to managers or organizational units). Plans, records, and then analyzes costs against 
the plan. Uses internal orders to plan, collect, and settle the costs of internal jobs and tasks. 
Monitors internal orders thr oughout their entire lifecycle using the SAP system, from initial 
creation, through the planning and posting of all the actual costs, to the final settlement and 
archiving. 
  
 Profitability Accounting 
Records costs and reven ues by market segment, which can be products and product groups, 
customers and customer groups, orders, or any combination of these or strategic business units, 
such as sales organizations or business areas, to calculate the contribution margin for each 
market segment.

 Purchase Requisition:  
A purchase request is a request or instruction to purchasing to procure a certain quantity of a 
material or a service so that it is available at a certain point in time. The purchasing department 
works with the purchasing request, decides on whether the re quirement of products can covered 
from stock or need to be procures externally. Approvals of the requisition will be setup in 
accordance with your requirements. 
 
 Source Determination:  
The Purchasing component helps you identify potential sources of supply  based on past orders 
and existing longer -term purchase agreements. This speeds the process of creating requests for 
quotation (RFQ’s). The system is capable of simulating pricing scenarios, allowing you to compare 
a number of different quotations. 
 
 Purchase Order Processing: 
The purchase order can be used for a variety of procurement purposes. You can procure materials 
for direct consumption or for stock. You can also procure services. The system checks the 
reminder periods you have specified and if necess ary automatically prints reminders or expediters 
at the predefined intervals. It also provides you with an up -to-date status of all purchase 
requisitions, quotations, and purchase orders. The purchase order will then be used to process a 
goods receipt in accordance with your purchasing polices. 
 
ii. Inventory Management

with Supply Chain accounting and is responsible for goods receipt s, goods issues and managing different 
fuel stock categories (available, blocked stock, in quality assurance and so on) and special stocks. SAP 
Inventory Management enables a summarized visibility of fuel stocks in the supply chain. The following 
diagram depicts the inventory management solution in SAP.

processing/packaging. The receipting process is tightly linked to the financials modules so as to

Plant Maintenance 
The Plant Maintenance component is being proposed for NOCMA  so as to process business transactions 
related to the management of technical assets such as equipment and machinery, vehicles and buildings. 
Included in the various transactions are capabilities to keep records of the technical assets, plan 
maintenance a nd execute maintenance for breakdown, and normal repair. Some of the key processes 
are as follows:- 
 
 Maintenance and Processing: - complete and reliable plant & equipment maintenance solution 
leads to higher system availability and reduced breakdown costs by means of preventive 
inspections and maintenance. Plant Maintenance allows for more transparent and simplified 
processes through integrated plant maintenance capabilities. Therefore, material and labor costs 
can be reduced while maintaining the same level of system quality, and costs for the use of 
external service providers can be optimized.  
 
 Technical Assets Management: -A piece of equipment is an individual object that is to be 
maintained independently. Each piece of equipment is managed independently in the system, so 
that you can: 
– Manage individual data from a maintenance perspective for the object  
– Perform individual maintenance tasks for the object 
– Keep a record of the maintenance tasks performed for the object 
– Collect and evaluate data over a long period of time for the object. 
 
 Preventive & Predictive Maintenance:  - Preventive maintenance work is performed to ensure 
optimal operation of equipment & avoid costly unplanned equipment failure or shutdown. This work 
may be done for legal/ regulatory reasons, or due to environmental protection requirements. This 
type of maintenance may also be done to comply with manufacturer recomm endations, or may be 
executed to ensure continued product quality. 
 
 Maintenance Planning & Scheduling: - Ensuring a high availability of objects in the long term is 
an important part of Plant Maintenance. Planned maintenance is used to avoid system breakdo wns 
or the breakdown of other objects, which - in addition to the repair costs - often result in much 
higher costs subsequently owing to production breakdown. There are many benefits in using 
planned maintenance within NOCMA. It is the generic term for inspections, preventive maintenance 
and planned repairs, for which the time and scope of the work can be planned in advance.  
 
 Work Order Management : - Orders form an important part of the detailed planning of tasks and 
their accompanying documentation in Plant Maintenance. A technician on site must execute tasks 
at a technical object. For this, materials, utilities and staff must be planned and costs  estimated. 
The order supports you with these tasks, since it primarily contains data for planning and executing 
tasks, which must be performed at the technical object in question.  
 
 Refurbishment:-You use this business process to perform the refurbishment  of equipment. The 
process is seamlessly integrated with the maintenance cycle. Technical assets (serialized 
equipment) are linked to the material master and can be managed in condition -based stocks. Any 
refurbishment activity performed influences the overall stock value of the material (equipment).

4.2 Technical Response  
 
4.2.1 General   
 
Web based and accessible from head office and site offices through NOCMA’s Local Area and Wide Area 
Networks (wired, wireless), with possibility to be accessed through intranet and internet.   
 
Response  
SAP has provided fully web-based assisted solution. All the applications in SAP are browser    based 
without the need for additional components. SAP Application Server can function both as a Web server 
and as a Web client. SAP solution is provided with a choice of clients, all of which are thin clients: GUI for 
Windows, GUI for Java, GUI for HTML and SAP Portal (SAP NP).SAP includes the embedded Application 
Server (AS), which embraces native Web technologies through a standards -based approach. SAP 
Internet Communication Manager (ICM) is a component of the SAP Application Server.  
 
WAN connectivity between NOCMA branch networks and to the Internet for SAP remote online support 
will be achievable by means of either dedicated link(s) or shared link(s) and fundamentally the Internet 
link is through an Internet Service Provider (ISP).  Various options, albeit inappropriate for this guide , are 
available for the WAN links namely Fibre Optic, VSAT, Radio, Leased Line, Cellular. The selection of 
which option is practical and appropriate is dependent on a number of factors which are best reviewed 
and decided on given individual Project requirements and budgets. However the illustration below serves 
to depict how some of the options can be pieced together 
 
Database driven, using a centralized relational Database Management System (DBMS), preferably SQL 
Server. Database system license should be provided by the consultant.  
 
Response  
SAP is compatible with database platforms such as MS SQL, Oracle, Sybase, MaxDB, DB2. While the 
database license will already be covered under the SAP Software license, the Operating system will have 
to be purchased and licensed separately by the client. Whilst SAP runs well on all the certified operating 
system and RDBMs TTCS would recommend Sybase Adaptive Server Enterprise Version 15 as the 
RDBMS for the SAP system because: 
 
 Sybase is an SAP database, which is optimized to run SAP products. 
 SAP is developed on Sybase and Suse Linux. This combination is tried and tested and SAP 
developments and testing are done on Suse Linux and Sybase. 
 It provides optimized integration with other SAP solutions and technologies like t he SAP HANA 
platform. 
 Sybase has a well -established and skilled workforce, offering infrastructure and development teams 
who are fully familiar with database architectures and Sybase products. 
 
All data is stored in a shared, central database, guaranteeing true, real-time integration. There is no need 
for batch interfaces to transfer data from one “module” to another. As a result, this ensures NOCMA true 
real-time integration across business processes 
 
System should support real time data capturing and update with central database. 
 
Response  
With the advent of distributed client–server computing SAP AG brought out a client–server version of the 
software called SAP R/3 (the "R" was for "Real-time data processing" and "3" was for "3-tier": 1) 
database, 2) application server, and 3) client (SAPgui)). This new architecture is compatible with multiple 
platforms and operating systems, such as Microsoft Windows or UNIX. This opened up SAP to a whole 
new customer base. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 34 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 34 of 275  
A newer version of the software, with revised technical architecture, was released in 2004, and renamed 
as SAP ERP Central Component (ECC). SAP came to dominate the large business applications market. 
The newest version of the product is SAP ECC 6.0 Enhancement Pack 7. 
SAP ECC is the core component of the SAP's Business Suite - a collection of applications including CRM 
and SCM among others. The combined complexity of the Business Suite, along with newer in -cloud 
competitors, has in recent years led SAP to invest heavily in simplification and massiv ely improved 
system response times, culminating in the announcement of the S/4 Simple Suite in February 2015. S/4 
has a single tenant architecture and is being built upon SAP's in-memory database technology stack 
(HANA) and will be available in a choice of in-cloud and on-premises deployment. The classic three-tier 
and database-agnostic architecture of R/3 is replaced with a two-tier architecture. 
 
 
Multi-level security features (User Authentication by username and password and user access level 
control for segregation of duties). Should allow concurrent user access for 15 users.  
 
Response  
SAP Security is administered at all levels which is the database, application and operating system levels. 
NOCMA users can only access these levels if they have the specific user names and passwords. And for 
the logged in users to be able to perform certain functions they have to have the right access privileges 
and rights. For example for one to perform some database related activities they need to possess the 
relevant database privileges. For SAP, database has its own users, the operating system has its own set 
of users and the application level has its own exclusive set of users. The application set of users cannot 
log into the database or operating system and the operating system users cannot log in to the application 
or database thereby further securing the SAP resources and components. 
 
The integrity of business data is also ensured by the assignment controlling the access assigned to users 
groups in SAP. Authorization profiles are generated which restrict the activities of users in the SAP 
System, depending on the activities in the roles. The generated roles can be assigned to a group of users 
such that all the members in that group will be having access to the functional areas defined within the 
authorization profile. 
 
SAP system has actually been implemented in organizations with as few as 4 concurrent users and as 
many as 10,000 concurrent users. The system is fully scalable and there are no limits to the number of 
employees that can be accommodated. One of SAP’s highest-volume performance benchmark is for 
47,528 concurrent users. This performance benchmark used 14 application servers  
 
Provide a user friendly, menu driven graphical interface in English language.  
 
Response  
SAP supports more than 30 languages which include English, Japanese, Portuguese, French and 
Germany. Therefore, English can be set as the default language and all menus and interfaces will be 
displayed in English. SAP GUI for HTML and windows offers end-user friendly, point & click oriented with 
same look and feel across the SAP proposed solutions. SAP Web UI is easy to learn, more intuitive to 
use and quicker to adapt with role-based personalization that enables users to determine the page layout, 
look and feel , and even which information they receive and how they receive it. Icon, menu layout etc. 
SAP ERP solution to an extent the look and feel is consistent in Graphical User Interface options. The 
look and feel of GUI, including the entry of command keys and data input, is also consistent across SAP 
applications. Customization is performed through the SAP Implementation Guide (IMG) and UI 
Configuration tool a highly table-driven, end-user friendly, point & click oriented way to customize any 
SAP system. SAP IMG tool allows NOCMA users to add columns, rows, or even new blocks, tables and 
graphs in which they can continue to freely drill down, drill up etc.

Modules should be able to work independently so that modular problems should not affect the whole 
system. 
 
Response  
System SAP has a modular structure; that is, it contains a number of modules accessible via one login to 
the SAP system.  
 

=== PDF PAGE 36 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 36 of 275  
SAP modules are widely known by their abbreviations. Modules are organized hierarchically; that is, they 
have sub modules (e.g., Fixed Assets FI-AA is a sub module of Finance FI). Some modules are 
extensions of existing modules, but are not officially a sub module (e.g., Fleet Management FM is an 
extension of Plant Maintenance PM). Hence, for two independent and self-reliant modules, the failure of 
one module does not imply the failure of the entire system. 
   

=== PDF PAGE 37 ===

the SAP system.  
 
SAP modules are widely known by their abbreviations. Modules are organized hierarchically; that is, they 
have sub modules (e.g., Fixed Assets FI-AA is a sub module of Finance FI). Some modules are

System should be able to provide a comprehensive audit trail of all transactions and user logon history. 
The system should support complex passwords and encryption, and support an en forced password 
policy. 
 
Response  
SAP audit reporting supports all activities tracking analysis on approvals, validations, reviews, requests, 
error, message, etc. are logged in SAP database to provide documentation of all activities relevant for 
audits. SAP solutions provide both a Security Audit Log (Transaction Log) and a System Log. The 
Security Audit Log complements the System Log. SAP Audit trail is capable of generating log trails, which 
contain details about any read / write access to sensitive data. Audit trail can be configured through 
Implementation Guide (IMG).Password controls can be set according to NOCMA requirements. There is 
no limit in terms of frequency of changes of passwords, but on parameter values, each will have its own 
permissible values. 
 
System should also provide an administrative module for user addition/modification/deletion/blocking, 
assignment of user roles 
 
Response 
To increase the security of the passwords in SAP systems, they are encrypted, and are only stored and 
transferred as hash values. The secure hash values, which are not backward-compatible, and which 
make reverse engineering attacks difficult, can be generated. By default, new systems generate two hash 
values: a backward-compatible value and a new value. However, the system can be configured so that 
only the new hash value, which is not backward-compatible, is generated. The Administrator can set the 
degree of backward compatibility with the profile parameter login/password_downwards_compatibility.  
 
User administration is done through user management console and profile parameter. The administrator 
can change, create, or delete users in the business areas for which they are responsible for any user for 
an indefinite or specified period of time. Profile parameters allow admin to permit effective date and time 
period for granting/ terminating system user access. You can remove authorization rights or lock 
respectively delete user accounts on a specific call date of employees leaving or retiring fr om all 
applications.

defined frequency.  
 
Response  
SAP provides backup options that are full, off-line backup, Full on-line backup, Incremental backup, Hot 
Failover, Cold failover and shared load through hardware partners (HP/IBM). SAP through hardware 
partners provides single server with Hot Back-up and Multiple Server backup and failover technology with 
degrading the system while databases and the system can be backed up while it is operational.

4.2.2 Finance and Accounting   
 
General 
Provide support for branch accounting 
 
Response  
The SAP system is an all-encompassing multi-faceted ERP system that allows a comprehensive overview 
and integration of transactions or activities between the main office, probably the head office, and all its 
inherent subsidiaries within the R/3 system. Branches or subsidiaries are defined as separate company 
codes in the SAP system and are mapped to the company code of the head office. Transactions, like for 
example vendor invoices, customer invoices can be raised at the branch or subsidiary level and upda ted 
in real time in both the head office accounts and the branch accounts.  
 
The head office can then make or receive payments on behalf of the branches. Taxes arising from the 
vendor or customer invoices for the two company codes are paid by the paying co mpany code, in this 
case the head office. Cross company code postings (like the posting of journal vouchers between 
different company codes in a single journal voucher), in this case between the head office and its 
branches in the SAP system can also take place within the SAP system. 
 
Provide multi-currency support 
 
Response  
The SAP system is an open ended system that allows organizations to define and post - transactions in 
different or multiple currencies. This is one of the functionalities of the SAP fin ancial Accounting module. 
Different currencies like the company code currency, group currency as well as currencies as defined or 
entered in a particular transaction can be input in the SAP system. The SAP system then converts the 
currency as entered by in the transaction using the exchange rate defined in that particular day. 
 
Also, with the new general ledger accounting (a functionality of the SAP Financial Accounting module), 
organizations are able to extract reports in different currencies. These curren cies could be currencies 
different from the company code currency, thereby allowing parallel reporting within the SAP system. This 
serves to generate various reports under different currencies. 
 
Perform electronic invoicing, receipting and cheque payment 
 
Response  
With the SAP Financial Accounting module, organisations are able to produce invoices to customers 
online. These customer invoices can be developed or customized within the SAP system to depict the 
logos of the organisation in question or the signatures of the issuing authority (which may need to be 
scanned and in scripted on the form for customer invoice printing in the SAP system).  
 
Receipts for cash payments from customers are also generated by the associated print program by the 
SAP Financial Accounting module which may later be watermarked in the system and issued to the 
customer who made the cash payment at the organisations cash desk.  
 
Cheques to suppliers or vendors may also printed which may also be both manual and electronic 
(cheques) payments. The manual cheques are linked to their payment documents in the SAP system and 
later updated in the SAP cheques register. Electronic cheques are automatically updated by the system in 
the electronic cheque register and associated withholding taxes levied on the payments are appropriately 
deducted from the payments. 
 
Should have the facility to import and export data from/to spreadsheet application (e.g. Microsoft Excel)  
 
Response

Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1

The SAP system allows users to import data from excel based documents. It also allows users to select 
excel spreadsheet as a destination to save reports in excel format. One can also download these reports 
into different other formats like HTML. 
 
Be able to upload and download financial documents e.g. letter of credits, invoi ces, loading reports, 
delivery notes etc for reference 
 
Response  
Within SAP, one is able to make use of Forms created within SAP. The forms refer to certain printouts for 
particular processes which will have been carried out in the system. These standard forms within SAP are 
customized to suite the relevant company. For example, an invoice printout will be customized with the 
company Logo. 
 
Enforce segregation of duties by limiting user access to sub-modules 
 
Response  
Using roles and authorizations, one is limited / restricted to data relevant to their organizational units and 
the components of data they are supposed to view/ use. If one is not supposed to have certain 
authorizations, this will be defined within the user’s roles. 
 
Ability to set up automated reminders for key deadlines such invoice due dates. 
 
Response  
SAP also supports the configuration of notifications, escalations, and reminders for user -defined 
workflows. Notifications are sent to the performer of a task when the task is created. They are used to 
notify the receiver about the new task in their work inbox. Reminders are e -mails sent to the performer of 
a task a short time before the end date or the due date of the task, in case the task is not completed. 
Escalations are notifications sen t to the performer's supervisor or to another responsible person, in case 
the performer misses the task completion due date, as specified in their work inbox.  
 
General Ledger 
Create accounting entities, business units, setup financial ledgers, sub-ledgers, accounting periods and 
define month end close rules 
 
Response  
In the SAP System, a legal entity can be created as a company code. Multiple companies can be created 
as company codes and all assigned to a ‘company’ which represents the Client.  Each compan y code is 
treated as an independent unit and numerous company codes can be created and assigned to one Group 
Company for consolidation purposes. The variant concept in SAP will enable us to define the accounting 
periods we will use and once the financial periods have been setup in the system, one can define period 
end/month end procedures can be defined. 
 
Ability to create multiple accounting periods per year, and manage the accounting periods  
 
Response  
Before data can be posted in SAP, one must define accounting periods and allowed accounting periods. 
Accounting period definitions determine the fiscal year(s) and posting periods that are available on the 
system; allowed accounting periods determine which posting periods are open for posting data. A 
company can define their fiscal calendars so that the system identifies data by a unique posting period. 
You only define the end date for the period. The system uses the business transaction date (posting date) 
to calculate to which fiscal period of a special purpose ledger the transaction data should be posted. 
 
Open and close financial accounting periods for business units, ledgers and sub-ledgers 
 
Response  

=== PDF PAGE 41 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 41 of 275  
One can specify which entities/company codes are open for posting in a posting period variant. Posting  
period variants are cross-company code and one has to assign them to their company codes. The posting 
periods are then opened and closed simultaneously for all company codes via the posting period variants. 
One can differentiate the opening and closing of posting periods by account type. This means that for a 
specific posting period, postings can be permitted to customer accounts, but not to vendor account  
 
Create, edit, correct, reverse and post journals including inter-unit journals between different business 
units 
 
Response  
Several company codes are involved in a cross -company code transaction. In a cross -company code 
transaction, the system posts a separate document with its own document number in each of the 
company codes. Individual documents are linked by a common cross-company code number. The system 
generates line items automatically (receivables and payables arising between company codes) in order to 
balance the debits and credits in each document. 
At times one company code makes purchases on beha lf of another company code or makes payment on 
behalf of another company code. This needs entries to be passed in both company codes. If cross 
company code settings are done, entry in one company code would generate the entry in the other 
company code also. These documents can be edited and reversed. 
 
Have a flexible and dynamic accounting structure 
 
Response  
The structure of Financial Accounting in SAP consists of Standard SAP functionality (e.g. database 
tables, user and expert transactions) that is delivered with the system and should not be changed unless 
there is a strong business need. Configuration settings set up in the system such as the chart of 
accounts. Master data, entered and maintained by business users, to be used repeatedly for many 
transactions over a long period of time. Lastly, there is transactional data which is entered or managed by 
business users and relating to a single business transaction. 
 
Have a robust chart of accounts structure that can allow creation of custom chart fields inc luding the 
income and cost analysis with cost centre codes 
 
Response  
The chart of accounts is shared by Financial Accounting as well as cost/revenue accounting. The items in 
a chart of accounts can be both expense or revenue accounts in Financial Accounting and cost or 
revenue elements in cost/revenue accounting. One can define the length of the G/L account numbers. 
The maximum length is ten characters. Internally, the system keeps the account numbers with a ten 
character length. The system pads purely numeric account numbers with zeroes from the left and 
alphanumeric account numbers from the right. 
 
Create and setup accounting rules for intercompany transactions and consolidation  
 
Response  
SAP Intercompany Reconciliation (ICR) provides you with pe riodic control over accounting documents 
that describe the accounting transactions within a corporate group. Designed to reduce the differences in 
corporate group consolidation, this application in Financial Accounting allows early analysis in the closing 
process to avoid differences altogether and to reduce the deadline pressure that normally arises during 
the end of a closing period. ICR operates on the level of companies and its trading partners. To avoid 
currency conversion differences, the documents ar e reconciled in the transaction currency. Both 
individual companies and their parent companies benefit from ICR. Individual companies benefit from 
paired documents because they need to ensure that their own documents from accounting transactions 
correspond to the documents of internal trading partners. This helps avoid delays and disputes when 
payments are processed. Their parent companies can then make a global check on the reconciliation 
results for all the companies. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 42 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 42 of 275  
 
Ability to create and maintain unlimited number of ledgers, with an option to include or exclude any of the 
ledgers for one or multiple consolidations. 
 
Response  
In SAP General Ledger Accounting, one can use several ledgers in parallel. This allows one to produce 
financial statements according to different accounting principles, for example. You create an SAP ledger 
for each of the general ledgers needed. 
 
 
Perform sub-system reconciliation and ledger reconciliation 
 
Response  
In SAP, one must specify a reconciliation account in the master record so that all postings made to a 
subsidiary ledger are also posted to the general ledger. The use of this eliminates the need for 
reconciliations between ledgers and sub ledgers 
 
Enforce security, journal entry and maintenance and an approval process by utilizing workflow 
 
Response  
SAP Business Workflow enables the design and execution of business processes within SAP application 
systems. The workflow functionality ensures that the right documents are taken to the right person at the 
right time. Workflow processes are delivered as content in the SAP Business Suite. Additionally, one can 
not only enhance those workflows provided by SAP, but they can also create their own workflows. SAP 
Business Workflow is at the heart of the SAP system. 
 
Interface with 3rd party systems to upload accounting entry data. 
 
SAP has various communication and integration technologies that “bind” different SAP ABAP and Java -
based applications, along with the third-party enterprise solutions, external vendors, and SAP support 
organization into an enterprise-wide SAP solution adding value and driving the business needs of an 
organization. Remote Function Call (RFC) is SAP’s communication interface. 
 
Ability to view multi-year historical data 
 
Response

has been archived. Data is archived once the required settings have been set in the system.

Provide the capability to calculate interest on overdue payments 
 
Response  
To calculate interest on arrears for overdue items in SAP, one will set the Interest Calculation Type 
indicator. This indicator controls whether the interest is calculated on the basis of overdue conditions. To 
ensure that interest is only calculated once for each open item, activate the Maintain master records 
indicator. In this case, a batch input session is created with the name specified in the Batch input session 
name field. When you process the session, the key date of the last interest run is entered in the custo mer 
master record. When you carry out the next interest run, the system ensures that interest is not calculated 
again for open items up to this key date.  
 
Should have a facility to record post dated cheques & guarantee deposits as a collateral to get the 
privileges of credit purchase 
 
Response  
Post dated checks are a common form of payment. One can use the SAP system to enter post dated 
checks receivable in the system as they arrive, and to process them as they become due.  
 
Setup payment schedules for invoices, define discount terms and due dates 
 
Response  
Payment terms in SAP define the parameters to derive the due date for the payment of an invoice, cash 
discount terms and periods, installment terms, default payment block/method that can be set etc. Usu ally 
baseline date is defined and derived from payment terms which is in turn used as the base for calculation 
of due date for payment. Fixed date payment terms are used when the due date for the payment is a 
fixed date.

Provide the capability to handle partial payments  
 
Response

Allocation of this partial payment to an open item is to be carried out. When posting, the system marks 
the document number of the original open item in the line item for partial payment. The  original open item 
and the partial payment remain open.

Cash and Bank Management 
Perform automated bank reconciliation 
 
Response  
System provides automatically as well as manually, transaction reconciliation facility for all receipts and 
payment entries in the bank statement and our bank book. It provides list of un -reconciled entries, for 
further decision making and matching. In SAP system the reconciliation process is known under two 
methods, Manual and Automatic. 
 
Every Bank account will have 4 General Ledger Accounts. First will be a Main GL for the bank, second 
will be “Incoming clearing” for all receipts, third will be “Outgoing Clearing” for all Payments and fourth will 
be “unreconciled account”. After the reconciliation process, payment and receipt entries in the incoming, 
outgoing and unreconciled GL will be transferred in to the bank Main GL.  
The balances in the main bank account will be equal to the bank statement amount.  
The bank book balance shall be equal to the net balance of the all four bank GLs put together.  
 
View, calculate, enter and store cash positions for business units, group business units or entire company 
 
Response  
SAP Treasury Risk Management generally ensures liquidity, optimizes finance costs and yields on 
financial assets. The liquidity aspect or effects are reflected in SAP Cash management module.  
 
The SAP Cash Management is used to view, calculate, enter and store cash flows and to ensure that a 
business unit, group or the entire company have sufficient liquidity to cover its payment obligations as and 
when they fall due.  
 
SAP Cash management has cash position and liquidity forecasting as its main tools. The cash position 
looks into short term cash movements in the banks accounts while liquidity forecasting concentrates on 
mid-term movements in the sub-ledger accounts. The cash position uses bank accounts, bank clearing 
accounts and memo records for one to two days period. Liquidity forecasting uses information on debtors, 
creditors for periods one to 24 weeks which are set as planned items in the system of outflows and 
inflows.  
 
Calculate daily cash position worksheet 
 
Response  
SAP cash management makes use of value dates in the daily cash position calculations to ensure 
payments are done precisely when they are required and from adequately funded bank accounts. When 
excess funds are reflected on the position worksheet, suitable investment avenues are explored for 
profitable deployment. Real time updates and seamless system integration makes decision making on 
cash positions easy in SAP. 
 
Below is a screenshot of report menus available in SAP Treasury and Risk management module to view 
positions and liquidity related information.

Fixed Assets 
Maintain a comprehensive asset register 
 
Response  
Asset Accounting in the SAP system is used for managing and monitoring fixed assets. In Financial 
Accounting, it serves as a subsidiary ledger to the general ledger, providing detailed information on 
transactions involving fixed assets. 
 
Setup multiple depreciation calculation methods for assets and display depreciation status for each asset. 
Also be able to make adjustments to depreciation for each asset 
 
Response  
The depreciation calculation method is the most important characteristic of the base method. The 
depreciation calculation method makes it possible to carry out the numerous different types of 
depreciation calculation in the system. Depending on how the depreciation calculation method is set up, 
the system determines which further control parameters need to be specified in the depreciation key. For 
example, when you choose the stated percentage depreciation calculation method, one will have to enter 
a percentage in the depreciation key. 
 
Transfer assets between business units and locations and create journal entries for asset transfers  
 
Response  
Within SAP, one can do asset transfers depending on the scenario at hand. For the individual companies, 
an intercompany transfer represents a retirement for the one company and an acquisition for the other. 
Using intracompany asset transfer, you transfer a fixed asset, or an asset component, to a different asset 
master record. The target asset has to be in the same company code as the sending asset. Intracompany 
transfer may be necessary for example, an asset was created in the wrong asset class. Since you cannot 
change the asset class in the asset master data, you have to transfer the asset to a new master record.  
 
Ability to change the depreciation methods / rates  
 
Response  

=== PDF PAGE 50 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 50 of 275  
Certain depreciation methods necessitate a changeover to another calculation method for mathematical 
reasons in order to depreciate the asset completely within the period of use. An example is the d eclining-
balance method of depreciation, which never results in a net book value of zero.  
Therefore, when one assigns calculation methods to a depreciation key, they can enter a changeover 
method. The changeover method specifies when the system should change over to a different calculation 
method (for example, Changeover when net book value percentage is reached). The changeover method 
also specifies the conditions under which the changeover takes place. One can also enter a net book 
value percentage for certain changeover methods.  
 
Budgeting 
Prepare, maintain, and monitor budgets. Import budget data, manage and enforce budget controls 
 
Response  
The functions within the Funds Management component support an organization in creating and 
executing budgets. The purpose of Funds Management is to budget all revenues and expenditures for 
individual areas of responsibility, to control future funds transactions in accordance with the distributed 
budget, and to stop the budget being exceeded. One can adapt the budget to changes in conditions by 
entering releases, supplements, returns, and transfers. 
 
Allow budgets inputted based on the branches or cost centres 
 
Response  
Funds Management enables an organization to: Control revenues and expenditures and thus control the 
funding of business transactions of an organization. They can closely control their bud get, with the 
following questions in mind: 
 What funds will the areas of responsibility receive?  
 Where do these funds come from (source of funds)? 
 What are the funds used for? (Use of funds) 
Control the finances of your organization by comparing commitment and actual values with current 
budget values.

sheet, cash flow statement, debtors’ analysis, creditors analysis, stock analysis, audit trail, bank 
reconciliation statements and others

The SAP system offers a number of reports that help analyze data with reference to a specific area of

reports within each of the following submodules- Accounts Payable, Accounts Receivable, Fixed Assets 
and the Bank and Cash sub module. 
 
Produce customized financial reports

other SAP application components to meet their specific reporting requirements.  
Many reporting requirements can be met using the standard reports provided by various SAP application 
components. However, if their reporting requirements are not fulfilled by SAP’s standard reports, they can 
use the Report Painter to quickly and easily define their own reports.

4.2.3 Payroll  
  
Allow set-up and maintenance of Payroll rules including pay periods/ dates, allowances and deduction tax 
rules 
 
Response  
The  SAP payroll engine is a multinational solution that integrates directly with the HR software from SAP 
allowing users to enter HR information that becomes immediately available to the payroll program for 
gross-to-net calculations. The Payroll driver is adjusted to take into account country specific rules and 
regulations. The payroll software can be integrated with SAP ERP Financials as well, which facilitates the 
efficient transfer of payroll results for the general ledger, controlling and bank  transfer activities, and other 
operational purposes. 
 
The Payroll component of Human Capital Management handles all payroll processes, supports current 
legal regulations and collective agreement specifications, and ensures compliance with regulatory 
changes. Payroll makes it easy to collect and manage all the information required for the delivery of high -
quality payroll services. It provides reliable, flexible, and exceptional performance. Features include 
Payroll Accounting, Tax, Social Insurance, Travel Expenses Administration, Incentive Wages, Posting to 
Accounting, and Forms.  
 
Payroll will give NOCMA the tools to efficiently complete day-to-day payroll activities via comprehensive 
payroll functionality. SAP provides organizations with comprehensive payroll functionality to support 
essential core payroll functions, direct deposit and transfers, off-cycle payrolls, retroactive adjustments, 
calculation of averages, assessment of time data, loans, garnishments, and year -end processing.

components of SAP.

The Proposed System must integrate Payroll and Human Resource Management records and must

Response  
The SAP system is an integrated system which supports integration amongst the different modules in 
SAP. SAP does not only support integration across modules, but it also supports integration within 
Modules. The SAP HCM module consists of various sub-modules responsible for the management of the 
HR functions. The Personnel Administration sub-module is the central data repository center for 
employee master data. Employee data can be created through maintaining the respect ive information 
categories (referred to as infotype in SAP) for the respective information. Some of the infotypes even 
though they are payroll relevant, they are all maintained through the PA sub-module. Any changes to 
payroll relevant infotypes will have an immediate effect on the payroll in real time.

Staff Claims/Pension/Benefits and Office accommodation, Office Security 
 
Response  
SAP Human Resources, Benefits Management (PA-BN) offers employers a flexible framework for 
creating and managing tailor-made Claims/ Benefit Programs that includes but not limited to death 
compensation, Pensions and grants for their employees. Its versatile configuration options enable 
employers to offer a diverse range of gratuities and to accommodate even complex plan types. Benefit 
Plan types are the logical grouping of different benefit plans. Each Plan type is identified by a unique 
identifier, which has a maximum of four characters e.g. 
 
 MEDI - Comes under Health Plan Category  
 DCAR - Comes under Flexible spending Accts  
 LIFE - Comes under Insurance Plans Category e.g Pensions 
 SAVE - Comes under Savings Plans Category e.g Savings 
 
Plan types are a control mechanism for enrolment, since the system does not allow an employee to enrol 
in more than one benefit plan per plan type. This allows NOCMA, for example, to offer a choice of regular 
health care from different providers under one plan type, without the risk of accidentally enrolling an 
employee in more than one of these benefit plans. Within each plan category (for example, Pension 
Plans) you should define one plan type for each sort of benefit plan that the employee is likely to e lect (for 
example, Life Insurance, Spousal Life, Supplemental Life). Thus an employee can elect a plan from the 
Life Insurance as well as from the Spousal Life plan type. 
 
The following are examples of plan types for each of the plan categories: 
1. Health Plans 
 General medical care 
 Dental care 
 Vision care plan 
 Supplemental life insurance 
 
2. Savings Plans 
 Retirement Pension plan 
 Savings plan 
 
SAP Employee Self Service will allow NOCMA employees to view and manage their benefits 
independently online. Employees have continual access to update information about their benefits, and 
any changes they make are immediately effective. The integration between Benefits Administration and 


=== PDF PAGE 66 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 66 of 275  
Personnel Administration will also allow NOCMA to maintain dependent and beneficia ry information for 
example spouse or children. The integration of data entered online therefore enables NOCMA to increase 
cost-efficiency and guarantee accuracy of data. 
 
The Beneficiary Eligibility Rules component within SAP HCM Benefits Management allows  the user to 
carry out the gratuity eligibility determination process for all employees. In this step, you define 
beneficiary eligibility rules to calculate and determine how much an employee or employer should 
contribute and whether an employee is eligible to claim for a certain benefit depending on the pay scale 
or which types of family member are eligible as beneficiaries. The contributions are automatically 
deducted from the employee’s earnings each time payroll is run 
 
With SAP ERP HCM compensation/ Payroll end-users can define the overall cost of the benefit 
programmes offered by an organisation thus enabling NOCMA to create budget structures for different 
employee categories, location, merit increases, performance bonuses and long -term incentives (LTIs). 
Thereafter you can allocate the budget pool of money (or the number of awards in the case of LTIs) to the 
different budget units in an organisation.

4.2.5 Petroleum Operations Management / Inventory Management of Petroleum Products  
 
General 
Integrated with the General Ledger, accounts payable, accounts receivable, procurement, production and 
maintenance e.t.c 
 
Response  
The SAP system has modules that integrate seamlessly through an account determination tool which 
links inventory transactions in Materials Management to general ledger accounts in Financial Accounting. 
The Valuation class in the material master allows the posting of stock values to general ledger accounts. 
The G/L accounts can be defined at valuation class level along with othe r parameters. The system has a 
valuation grouping code which combines the valuation areas having same business properties for the 
account determination. This reduces number of entries to be created for automatic account determination 
for the stock postings . Below is an illustration of one example where automatic postings are set in the 
SAP system: 
 
 
 
Establish Codes for each depot/ reserve location where petroleum products will be kept, and also codes 
for products, transactions, customers 
 
Response  
The SAP system has the ability to create reserve locations where petroleum products will be kept. This 
locations will be defined as storage locations in the system and each location will have a unique code 
assigned to it. These locations will also have physical addresses defined. The material master in SAP has 
the ability to maintain unique products codes. The Customer master also stores information for specific 
customers and each customer is assigned a unique code. Below is an illustration of how storage locatio ns 
will be defined : 
 
 
 
Enforce segregation of duties by limiting user access to sub-modules and access rights  
 
Response  
User administration in the SAP system is done through user management console and profile parameter. 
The administrator can change, c reate, or delete users in the business areas for which they are 
responsible for any  user for an indefinite or specified period of time. Profile parameters allow admin to 
permit effective date and time period for granting/ terminating system user access. You can remove 
authorization rights or lock respectively delete user accounts on a specific call date of employees leaving

Importation 
Capture product supplier and product details, including supplier account code.  
 
Response  
SAP Materials  management has a number of master data that it uses for its operations namely the 
material master (to capture product details) and the vendor master (to capture supplier details).  
 
Capture product supplier 
The vendor master contains information about the product suppliers that an enterprise deals with. This 
information is stored in individual vendor master records. A vendor master record contains the suppliers 
name and address, as well as data such as: 
 
 The currency used for ordering from the vendor 
 Terms of payment 
 Names of important contact persons (sales staff) 
 
Since, to the accounts department, vendors are generally creditors (accounts payable), the vendor 
master record also contains accounting information, such as the relevant control account (reconci liation 
account) in the general ledger. Therefore, the vendor master record is maintained by both Accounting and 
Purchasing. The purchasing data pertaining to a supplier must have previously been captured before you 
can order from the supplier. The account ing data pertaining to a supplier must have previously been 
captured before you can enter the supplier's invoices in the system for payment.  
Capture Product Details 
 
The material master contains descriptions of the products a company procures, produces, an d stores. 
The material master is the central repository of product information (such as stock levels) for the 
company. The integration of all product data in a single database eliminates redundant data. The data 
stored in the material master is used by Inv entory Management, as well as by other components such as 
Purchasing, Materials Planning and Invoice Verification. 
 
All products that the company uses are captured in the system, if it happens that you need to procure a 
particular product for the first tim e it can easily be captured into the system by the Super user in 
Procurement. The Product is captured in the system with details like:- 
 
 Accounting: Valuation and costing/price calculation information. Examples: Standard price, past 
and future price, and current valuation. 
 Materials planning and control: Information for material requirements planning (MRP) and 
consumption-based planning/inventory control. Examples: Safety stock level, planned delivery 
time, and reorder level for a material. 
 Purchasing: Data provided by Purchasing for a material. Examples:  Purchasing group 
(group of buyers) responsible for a material, over- and  under-delivery tolerances, and the order 
unit. 
 Storage: Information relating to the storage/warehousing of a materi al. Examples: unit of issue, 
storage conditions, and packaging dimensions. 
 Forecasting: Information for predicting material requirements. Examples: How the material is 
procured, forecasting period, and past consumption/usage. 
 Sales and distribution: Inform ation for sales orders and pricing. Examples: Sales price, minimum 
order quantity, and the name of the  sales department responsible for a certain material. 
Every material that is included in stock is defined in a material master record and identified by a  material 
number. However, it is possible for the purchasing department to order materials (or services) for which 
no material master record is defined. These are usually products that are only purchased once. These 

=== PDF PAGE 75 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 75 of 275  
materials, which do not have a material number, are described by a text and an assignment to a material 
group, both of which are entered in the purchase order. They are not included in stock; instead, when 
they are received, they are posted to consumption. 
  

=== PDF PAGE 76 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1

SAP R/3 system has a Transportation Component which enables planning and processing and shipment 
of products. This component facilitates the flow of information from the shipper to  NOCMA and to any 
service agents who are involved in the business transaction.  
 
The transportation component includes basic transportation processing functions:  
 Transportation planning and shipment completion 
 Shipment costs calculation 
 Shipment costs settlement 
 Shipment costs calculation for individual customers 
 Billing of customer freight 
 Service agent selection 
 Management of means of transport and utilities 
 Follow-up and supervision of shipments 
 Management of shipment costs 
These transportation functions can handle planning and processing for both inbound and outbound 
shipments. NOCMA can control and monitor the entire transportation process from the planning stage to 
goods issue at your shipping point (for outbound shipments)

Capture product inspection data including product quality and quantity information up on receiving product  
 
Response  
The SAP system has a quality inspection component to determine whether your company’s product 
consistently meets defined quality requirements. In a quality inspection, you inspect a material or product, 
using inspection spe cifications that have been predefined in the inspection planning component. The 
inspection results document the current quality of a material or product.  
Quality inspection covers the following important functions:  
 
 Inspection lot creation  
In the Quality Management component, you process quality inspections on the basis of inspection lots . If 
you want to inspect a specific quantity of a material or a piece of equipment, an inspection lot must be 
created in the system. Once an inspection lot  has been created, you can inspect the goods, record the 
inspection results or defects, and complete the inspection with a usage decision  
 
 Results recording  
In the Results Recording component, you record and process results for inspection characteristics.  The 
inspection lot is the reference object in results recording.  The recorded inspection results document the 
quality of the inspected product, and provide the basis for creating batch specifications and inspection 
certificates. You can use the recorded result data to make evaluations for quality control purposes. 
 
 Defects recording  
When you process an inspection lot, you can record the results for the inspection in the form of 
characteristic values or defects. A defect is any property or attribute of a material, product or process that 


=== PDF PAGE 78 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 78 of 275  
does not meet the inspection characteristic specifications. You record the defects with the help of 
predefined defect codes maintained in the inspection catalogs.

shipping point, the route, and the loading group of the material. 
 
 
Maintain product receipt records and manage goods received information;  
 
Response

specific storage  locations.  Information t hat is captured during a goods received process include the 
product description, quantity, goods recipient, storage location e.t.c 
 
Good Receipt  
A goods receipt  is the physical inbound movement of Products into the warehouse. It is a goods 
movement that is used to post goods received from external vendors or from in -plant production. All 
goods receipts result in an increase of stock in the warehouse. 
 
Some kinds of goods receipts available in SAP Materials management are: 
 Goods receipt with reference to a purchase order 
 Goods receipt with reference to a production order 
 Goods receipt with reference to a delivery 
 Other goods receipts (without reference)

has been received if there is a difference e.g. due to breakages it can be indicated as a loss or th e 
supplier might be notified that they supply the remaining balance

Automatic trigger alerts for ordering when products are below safety stock or re -order level 
 
Response  
SAP materials management has a feature referred to as  Material resource planning tool. This tool 
ensures that the company is always having enough stocks. With this feature you configure a re -order 
point for a product and when the stock level drops to the configured re -order value the system 
automatically triggers the creation of purchase documents to procure the required product. The reorder 
level is maintained within  a products material master as outlined below: 
  
  
 
 
Automatically create reorder advice  based on the minimum stock level, safety stock and lead time for 
products. 
 
Response  
In the SAP system the purpose of the safety stock is to prevent material shortage. To determine the 
safety stock level, you must specify the risk of a material shortage and the desired service level. The 
Safety stock is included in the reorder level quanti ty specified in the material master. If the stock falls 
below this level, the system flags the material for requirements planning by creating a planning file entry 
based on the configuration. In reorder point planning, an entry is only created in the plann ing file and the 
net requirements calculation is only carried out if stock levels fall below the reorder point. Sending of 
notification when items fall below reorder point is not a standard functionality but it can however be easily 
customized to suit the requirements of NOCMA

stock transfer such that a report can be easily displayed based on specified selection parameters.

Reconcile daily stock movements 
 
Response  
 
Stock Movement 
Goods movements include both "external" movements (goods receipts from external procurement, goods 
issues for sales orders) and "internal" movements (goods receipts from production, withdrawals of 
material for internal purposes, stock transfers, and transfer postings). For each goods movement a 
document is created which is used by the system to update quantities and values and serves as proof of 
goods movements. Goods rece ipt/issue slips can be printed to facilitate physical movements and monitor 
the individual stocks in the warehouse. 
In the system you can easily go back to check the goods movement that took place for a particular stock 
material. The documents created for each material movement (material documents) help track each 
movement. They show the date, the movement type (e.g. from warehouse to cost center for consumption, 
the material being used, where it is coming from, the stock type, the accounts evolved and any other 
reference  documents that were used. It to further track and be assured of our stock the 
system allows you to actually do a physical inventory in the warehouse or storage location of your

Response

service provider. An invoice is normally created after the goods receipt or service performance has been 
confirmed. It includes general invoice information , such as total amount, total tax, freight costs, vendor 
and invoice recipient, and detailed information (header information, item information, and approval 
preview).

Create request for quotation which is used to quote or print procurement requests which will be 
dispatched to various suppliers in printed form. 
 
Response  
The Request for quotation (RFQ) in the SAP system is used as an initial step in a vendor selection 
process.  The business sends out RFQ to the vendors and the vendors respond back with Quotations 
with prices.   Next step is quotation evaluation followed by issue of a p urchase order for the 
material/services needed. An RFQ has details such as the product name, quantities, quotation deadlines

Assignment of tasks and follow ups 
 
Response 
Certain industries have to perform comprehensive extensive ma intenance tasks repeatedly for highly 
complex technical structures (for example, aircraft maintenance, renovation of a refinery, and so 
on).Maintenance tasks of this type are usually mapped in the SAP System components Plant 
Maintenance (PM), Customer Serv ice (CS), and Project System  (PS). These maintenance tasks are 
activities that are repeated in cycles in either the same or a similar way, therefore you can define 
standard structures as templates (for example, standard projects, networks, maintenance plan s, and task 
lists). One can derive operative projects from standard projects and networks, and orders from standard 
maintenance plans and task lists. 
 
Close out projects after completion

check so many parameters before being closing the project. Once that is done, settlement is one of the 
process which transfers the actual cost sitting o n either network activities or WBS which are originated 
from various sources.

4.2.9 Document Management 
Document creation, uploading and downloading of letter of credits, product loading instructions, and 
loading reports, at NOCMA head office and site offices 
 
Response  
The proposed Electronic Document Management System (EDMS) for NOCMA is SAP Document 
Management. SAP DMS (Document Management System) is a cross application component of SAP ECC 
that provides robust document, content and digital asset management capabilities. SAP's Document 
Management System can help NOCMA improve their efficiency and streamlin e decision -making 
processes. 
 
SAP DMS enables the capture and management of electronic documents, CAD models and drawings, 
MS-Office files, scanned images, multi -media files, and any other electronic files that should be stored 
and controlled in a secure e nvironment. SAP DMS provides version and revision control, integrated 
workflow capabilities, full text retrieval and search functionality, and linkage with SAP business objects 
such as Bill of Materials, material masters (parts), process plans, engineering  change masters, and 
production orders. SAP DMS supports archiving and SAP classification schemes as well as third -party 
archiving repositories. SAP DMS provides integrated viewing and redlining capabilities.  
 
SAP DMS functionality will enable Users to do the following 
 Store and retrieve all of a company's important documents in a central repository, which is 
immediately accessible and completely integrated with the business processes mapped in the SAP 
system. 
 Check in and check-out documents as needed. 
 Have multiple parts .and versions of a single document for tracking purposes.  
 Index all documents for easy and comprehensive searches. 
 Search all documents by keywords in a long text of a document. 
 Organize all documents in a structured and hierarchical way. 
 Track all documents by their statuses and have document statuses control business functions.  
 Approve documents by individual digital signatures and also by a series of digital signatures.  
 Distribute (manually and by SAP Workflow) documents to relevant stak eholders. 
 Access documents on the Internet (Web Documents). 
 Have the option of working with SAP DMS via SAP Easy DMS a Microsoft Windows Explorer 
interface. 
 Control accessibility of documents to only the relevant stakeholders by authorization options  
 
Document management (DMS) in the SAP system will offer the following advantages to NOCMA  
 If you link document management to computerized development and production systems, you avoid 
data redundancy, maintain consistency of data, and minimize the workload inv olved in entering and 
updating your data. 
 In order to use large databases to the full, you need to be able to exchange data quickly and 
securely. You can access your data directly using electronic search tools, or find documents using 
known parameters. You  can also search for and display documents (original application files) via the 
Internet/intranet. By reducing access time and the workload involved in routine tasks, you can lower 
your costs considerably. 
 You can use document distribution to distribute do cuments that are managed using the document 
management (DM) functions either manually or automatically according to company -specific 
processes. This ensures that the employees responsible or external partners can view or process up -
to-the-minute information. 
 Stricter product liability laws mean that it is becoming increasingly important to archive documents 
during the life cycle of a product. The quality of a product includes the production process and the 
whole organizational environment, as well as the pr oduct itself. A company that wishes to be certified 
for quality management (ISO 9000 to 9006) can only meet strict quality requirements by using high -
performance document management functions. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 101 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 101 of 275  
Document management in the SAP system meets all these complex requirements. In addition to 
managing documents, it also coordinates document processing. You can automate the entire life cycle of 
a document: from document creation to document storage, from access to update. Each document can 
be accessed immediately from any computer in the network.

Response

to meet one’s requirements. All the reports can be sorted and printed according to NOCMA’s selection 
criteria.  
 
The Key reports available in the SAP Payroll system include amongst others: 
 
 Payroll Journal 
 Remuneration Slips and other reports which can be customized to specific user requirements   
 Payments and deductions report which is a breakdown of the various payments and deductions per 
period. Analysis of the various payment and deductions can also be done.  
 Bank Details which shows the payment method as well as the employee bank details.  
 Wage type report-this allows for a variance analysis to be done. 
 Payroll Changes report

 Creation of posting documents of payroll data to other modules such as Financials and Controlling. 
Payroll results are interfaced with GL by mapping each earning and deduction with its respective GL 
account. This mapping is done using symbolic accounts which read the respective allowances and 
deductions and post them to the correct GL accounts. When usi ng SAP Financials the results are 
posted automatically to the Financials GL accounts. When using a third party accounting system a file 
can be exported out of the system in a format that can be imported into the third party system. SAP 
Payroll System has a post payroll results to FI function that facilitates for all these processes.

developing queries and for ad-hoc reporting. InfoSet Query is suitable for reporting in all areas of the SAP 
R/3 system. When InfoSet Query is used in HR for ad-hoc reporting, the name Ad-Hoc Query is used 
instead of InfoSet Query.

 Basic List        - Simple reports

provides special views of logical databases. They determine which fields of a logical database can be 
evaluated in queries.
