4.2.12 Data Migration 
 
The consultant shall: 
Migrate accounting and payroll data from the current SAGE Pastel Evolution and Premier systems 
respectively, to the new system for business continuity. 
 
Response  
Data required by SAP, such as master data, around which transactions will be generated will be specified 
and sourced from the existing legacy systems. In addition transactional data as agreed, guided by cut 
over points and period will also be identified and sourced from the current  systems. 
 
It is crucial for the teams to thoroughly prepare data and systems before a migration takes place. In 
particular, landscape analysis is an important part of preparing for a data migration. It provides an 
overview of the source and target systems , enabling the project team to understand how each system 
works and how the data within each system is structured. SAP uses LSMW, BAPIs and IDOCs to migrate 
data from legacy systems to SAP. All static data can be migrated into SAP. 
 
Static data such as Cus tomer details, Supplier details, Bank account details, Inventory Item details, GL 
account Codes form a crucial group of data referred to as Master Data in the SAP solution, TTCS will 
therefore require this data in the new SAP solution and its source will b e the current systems from where 
it will be migrated. Other data such as account balances will also be required as they provide a starting 
point or reference point in time from which future transactions will impact. Such data will therefore be 
migrated by TTCS as well.

4.3 Hardware / Infrastructure Specifications  
 
4.3.1 Introduction 
The typical full-scale SAP system landscape consists of Development, Quality Assurance and Production 
Environments for each SAP solution being implemented and several individual workstations connected to 
these servers in a Virtual Private Network (VPN) set up via either Local Area Network (LAN) and/or Wide 
Area Network (WAN) connectivity. A Solution Manager System is mandatory for any SAP system 
landscape.  Additional server(s) for Disaster Recovery purposes is/are also recommended especially for 
larger-scale and mission -critical operations, although the DRP set up itself usually falls outside the 
installation scope and has to be negotiated and implemented separately unless stated otherwise in the 
contracts.   
 
4.3.2 Overview of the Hardware Requirements 
SAP system is supported by virtually all of the leading hardware vendors. One can choose from a whole 
range of potential hardware platforms from the major players in the hardware market including: IBM, Dell, 
Hewlett Packard, Sun Systems, Fiejitsu and EMC. SAP is also supported on the processors such as 
Xeon, Itanium, Opteron, POWER and SPARC. The hardware of choice should be durable and scalable. 
TTCS recommends IBM servers based on track record, durability and scalability.  
 
SAP Server Requirements 
The Sizing below is mainly dependent on the number of users, the number of modules and a retention 
period of 5 years. The sizing might need to be revised after the blueprint phase when the full scope of the 
project has been determined.  
 
Number of users For NOCMA: 
Minimum of 15 
 
Volumes of transaction records 
N/A   
 
System utilization 
Approximately 60%

Area Networking (SAN). The disk space does not take into consideration RAID configuration. 
 
The ideal infrastructure to accommodate all the logical servers will be a virtualized environment. The 
actual number of servers will then depend on the size of the servers to be acquired. There should be a 
dedicated link between the Production site and the Disaster recovery site. The DRP Implementation is

=== PDF PAGE 114 ===

Backup Requirements 
The database is always subject to the risk of damage and might be lost or become corrupted. As part of a 
disaster recovery plan, backup of the SAP systems should always be given high priority and thus a sound 
backup plan should be in place. The Servers to be used for SAP should be connected to either a tape 
library or a network backup device. 
The Backup Solution to be used depends on the Backup Strategy that is enshrined NOCMA 's  IT policy, 
however TTCS recommends a Backup Tape Library which allows the backups to be stored offsite. Back 
up is mandatory regardless of whether a Disaster recovery Server is there or not.  
 
 
 
Disaster Recovery Considerations 
Considering the size and importance of the New System, it is mandatory to have a Disaster recovery site. 
The DR server must not be housed at the same site/building as the PRD server; ideally it should be at a 
site within a radius of 5 to 20 Km’s from the main production site but wit h the two sites linked via a reliable 
VPN of 1Mbps minimum bandwidth dedicated link. The Disaster Recovery process can be manual, semi -
automatic or automatic depending on the choice selected. The standard Implementation does not include 
Disaster recovery implementation and this will have to be negotiated separately.   
 
RAID Configuration Option Consideration 
It is not mandatory to have Disk Mirroring and/or advanced RAID configurations for the Development and 
Quality Assurance systems but it is highly recom mended to use RAID 5 on all production Systems. The 
implication is that the Hard Disk Drive space requirements (and ultimately costs) increase considerably 
but the benefits are; High Availability of the system, better performance, greater speeds, greater 
reliability, minimized downtimes on disk failure amongst others. 
 
Server Environment considerations 
All the servers must be housed in ideal, dust -free and air -conditioned room/rooms as specified by the 
equipment manufacturer. In addition to acquiring the co rrectly sized servers, special attention must be 
given to the power rating and consumption aspects of these servers. During the acquisition process it is 
imperative to crosscheck and ensures that the power source intended for the servers is of the correct 
capacity and rating as specified by the equipment manufacturer. Below are some of the Server room 
requirements: 
 
1. Room Size: Dimensions of the server room should be a minimum of 3m (length) x 2.5m (width) x 
2.5m (standard room height). This is to allow for the racks as well as working space.  
 
2. Primary power supply : Power supply should be calibrated to the exact specification of the 
manufacturer and should be sourced direct from wall or floor mounted sockets as opposed to 
extension sockets. There should be a minimum of 4 (four) such wall or floor mounted sockets 
reserved and available for the server equipment. The server room will need to be adequately 
earthed.  
 
3. Alternate power supply : It is recommended to have Uninterruptible Power Supply (UPS) and a 
standby generator to cater for cases of extended primary power losses.  
 


=== PDF PAGE 115 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 115 of 275  
4. Air-conditioning: (Including humidity control); with the aid of an approved air -conditioning unit 
temperatures inside the server room should be kept between 16 – 24 Degrees Celsius and a 
humidity range of 40 – 55% with a maximum dew point (condensation) of 15 Degrees Celsius.  
 
5. Dust and direct sunlight: The server room must be dust -proof and the positioning of the servers 
should be such that at no time are they in direct sunlight rays 
 
6. Fire hazard alert and control:  There must be adequate fire hazard alarms and fire control 
facilities inside and around the server room such as automated Carbon Dioxide extinguishers. 
These facilities should be regularly checked and maintained for proper functio ning  
 
7. Flooring and cabled network : Ideally the server room should have a raised floor with a steel 
base plate strong enough to support a minimum weight of 5 tones i.e. 5000 Kilograms. The raised 
floor will allow for both power and network cabling to run underneath it. The height (raise) of the 
floor can be adjustable but with a minimum of 15centimetres off the ground. The server room 
must have a cabled network set -up as opposed to wireless specifically for the servers. Preferably 
the network should be mes hed to provide for failover. The floor can be wooden or tiled (ceramic) 
but certainly not carpeted due to static electricity generated from carpeting.  
 
8. Physical access control:  Entry into and exit out of the server room should be controlled and 
monitored. We recommend a state of the art access control system to be installed, the likes of 
Impro IXP220Online Access Control. Additionally a recording of movements within and around 
the server room should be monitored via CCTV 
 
 
4.3.3 Overview of Software Requirements 
 
Operating system and database options 
As in the case of Hardware, SAP system is capable of running on a variety of Operating Systems (OS) 
namely Microsoft Windows Server, LINUX, UNIX, AIX and Database platforms such as MS SQL, Oracle, 
Sybase, MaxDB, DB2. While the database license will already be covered under the SAP Software 
license, the Operating system will have to be purchased and licensed separately by the client. Whilst SAP 
runs well on all the certified operating system and RDBMs TTCS would recomme nd Sybase Adaptive 
Server Enterprise as the RDBMS for the SAP system. 
 
TTCS highly recommends that System Administrators be trained in the Operating systems and the 
database platforms that they will be expected to work with prior to go live.  
Benefits of SUSE Linux Enterprise Server for SAP Applications 
 It is SAP’s own development platform for Linux, SAP and SUSE have a close joint testing and 
development relationship that starts at the SAP Linux Lab in Germany.  
 SUSE Linux Enterprise Server for SAP applicat ions is the only operating system platform 
optimized for all mission-critical SAP NetWeaver software solutions and appliances. 
 It is validated and certified by SAP and is, therefore, uniquely positioned to support all of your 
SAP software solutions including appliances.  
 I has high availability with cluster support that gives customers built -in scalability for large 
systems and the potential for third-party integration 
 It supports large workloads. SUSE Linux Enterprise Server includes page cache limit, a ke rnel 
parameter to allow SAP administrators to optimize the kernel's paging behavior. This feature is 
crucial for SAP applications to gain performance improvements. 
 SUSE Linux Enterprise Server for SAP Applications can be virtualized with SUSE, VMware, and 
Citrix virtualization solutions, all supported and certified by SAP. 
 
 
Advantages of Using SAP Sybase ASE Database 
SAP Sybase ASE is the preferred database for transaction environments, especially for SAP ERP and 
other SAP Business Suite applications. 

=== PDF PAGE 116 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 116 of 275  
 Sybase is an SAP database, which is optimized to run SAP products. 
 SAP is developed on Sybase and Suse Linux. This combination is tried and tested and SAP 
developments and testing are done on Suse Linux and Sybase. 
 It provides optimized integration with ot her SAP solutions and technologies like the SAP HANA 
platform. 
 Sybase has a well -established and skilled workforce, offering infrastructure and development 
teams who are fully familiar with database architectures and Sybase products.

4.3.4 Network Requirements 
 
Since the SAP -based operations will be geographically spread throughout a branch network the 
interaction between the workstations and the SAP servers will have to be facilitated by both LAN and 
WAN connectivity in a VPN set-up. 
 
However for purposes of the SAP Router application it is mandatory  that the client have an Internet link 
(refer to the section on SAP Router below). The minimum bandwidth for the WAN link(s) should be 512 
Kbps but the TTCS recommendation is 1 Mbps. 
 
LAN Connectivity 
Interaction between and among the Servers and workstati ons is facilitated through a Local Area Network. 
There are 2 fundamental alternative platforms for LAN connectivity, that is wired and wireless.  
 
The choice to use one or the other or both is primarily dependent on the Geographic’s and mobility 
requirements of the workstations connected onto the LAN, therefore for a LAN predominated by fixed 
desktop workstations the natural choice would be wired (i. e. cable connections) whereas if laptops are 
predominantly in use and the people move around with them a lot then a wireless LAN would be the 
preferred option. In a mixed operating environment it is very practical and at times advisable to have a 
hybrid of wired and wireless connectivity.  
 
Where a wired LAN is considered appropriate TTCS recommends it be implemented based on the 
concept of structured cabling technology (see below). 
 
 
 
 
The main functional units within a typical wired LAN are; 1 or more sw itches (TTCS recommends Cisco), 
a Patch panel and patch cords, CAT 5E cabling (TTCS recommends Molex) and RJ45 connectors, while 
the non -functional but essential components are the Network Cabinet and Cable Trucking. Below is a 
sketch diagram depicting a typical hybrid of a wired and wireless LAN. 
 


=== PDF PAGE 118 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 118 of 275  
 
 
WAN Connectivity 
WAN connectivity between branch networks and to the Internet for SAP remote online support is 
achievable by means of either dedicated link(s) or shared link(s) and fundamentally the Internet l ink is 
through an Internet Service Provider (ISP).  
 
Various options, albeit inappropriate for this guide, are available for the WAN links namely Fibre Optic, 
VSAT, Radio, Leased Line, Cellular. The selection of which option is practical and appropriate is  
dependent on a number of factors which are best reviewed and decided on given individual Project 
requirements and budgets. However the illustration below serves to depict how some of the options can 
be pieced together.

SAP Router Consideration 
The SAP Router application program is the vehicle through which SAP clients are connected to the SAP's 
remote customer support facility. The application is installed on a PC or Server (usually Solution 
Manager) and connected to the SAP support center through the Internet via Secure Network 
Communication (SNC) or Virtual Private Network (VPN).

more complex to implement.

5. Approach, Methodology and Work Plan for performing the Assignment  
 
5.1 Approach and Methodology  
 
Twenty Third Century Systems proposes an implementation approach based upon our proven experience 
with similar projects. At the National Oil Company of Malawi (NOCMA)  the project will be managed and 
executed using the Accelerated SAP (ASAP) methodology and toolset, which was develope d specifically 
to manage SAP solution implementations.  
 
The methodology toolset includes a project plan, step -by-step roadmap, and project accelerators tailored 
to implementing SAP Solutions. Each phase has a set of deliverables that are produced during t he 
duration of the period and serve as the input to subsequent phases. Each deliverable provides a list of 
outputs and methods that are used to produce the deliverables. The Figure below is a summary of the 
ASAP Methodology. 
ASAP Methodology 
 
 
The ASAP methodology delivers the following phases: 
a) Project Preparation Phase 
 
 
The objective of this phase is to provide initial planning, organization and preparation for the 
implementation.  
The project sponsor, project manager and all team leaders must sign off  a project charter. The project 
charter defines the scope of the project, methodology to be used, detailed implementation plan and the 
rules of implementation. It is the result of the planning phase.

c) Realization Phase 
 
 
The objective of the Realization phase is to implement the information, business process and technical 
requirements based on the Business Blueprint developed during the previous phase.  
The final configured system  will reflect NOCMA organization and master data as well as a fully integrated 
process flow through the system. A playback of business processes to project team members as well as 
other key users of each business process allows for feedback and further val idation of the business 
blueprint. We assume that NOCMA will accept best practices as identified by SAP in order to ensure 
achievement of project time frames. 
 
The business process procedures are configured in concert with the development of reports, forms, end 
user procedures (EUP), testing scenarios and security profiles.  
To support the SAP implementation, the technical team will complete the design, development, and 
testing of data conversion and interface programs. We will work closely with the NOCMA Legacy Experts 
to make sure we bring across the appropriate data. 
 
The process team will compile User Acceptance Procedures (ATP’s), which record testing results of all 
business processes defined in the Blueprint document. Users will use the ATP docume nt to test the 
system and ensure that it meets their business requirements and confirm that it will function in their 
business environment. If users are happy with the system, they will confirm this by signing off the system.  
 
The process teams will be required to demonstrate the system to the steering committee. The steering 
committee will also be required to carry out a review and sign off the systems if they are satisfied with the 
system as would have been demonstrated by the process teams. 


=== PDF PAGE 124 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 124 of 275  
If users or the steering committee raises any issue, these must be rectified and the system demonstrated 
again. Users and the steering committee can also make a conditional sign off that they accept the system 
on condition that the issues they raised (listed on the sign off) will be resolved. It is the duty of the project 
manages to ensure that issues are resolved promptly.

d) Final Preparation 
 
The objectives of the Final Preparation phase are to complete final system testing, train End Users and 
cut over both the data and the system to a production environment. Final system testing consists of 
testing conversion, procedures, transactions, custom  programs, interfaces, volume testing and 
conducting final user acceptance testing. Another purpose of this phase is to finalize the Go -Live plan(s), 
in particular the data conversion schedule, audit procedures, help desk procedures, and field support.  
 
Prior to end user training, the training team will be responsible for acceptance testing and validation of 
training materials. Also, the executives and stakeholder groups are involved in employee preparation 
activities. Organizational infrastructure is modi fied or put into place to support new business process. The

final step in this phase is to approve the system and the readiness of NOCMA to go live and to officially 
turn on the SAP system.  
 
Users, process teams and the steering committee must sign off th e live scenario tests. A sign off will be a 
confirmation of accepting the system. This is done when users, the process team and the executive are 
satisfied that the system meets the business requirements after the scenario testing.

Project Management Method 
ASAP methodology project management tools will be used throughout the project. ASAP offers 
comprehensive tools, which assist in planning and implementing SAP. These tools include 
comprehensive documentation, resources to be used and a suggested implementation plan. ASAP 
Methodology tools also assist in drawing up project charter, scheduling of workshops with suggested 
agenda and issues to be discussed. The tools offer a useful and comprehensive guide throughou t the 
whole life cycle of the project. 
 
Project Planning Documentation 
The project plan will be developed using the ASAP methodology. ASAP provides a planning tool template 
based on MS Project. 
 
Quality Management 
ASAP methodology provides for quality checks at the end of each phase. The ASAP Accelerators provide 
guidance on what has to be checked and signed off at the end of each stage. There will be a sign off at 
the end of each stage before proceeding to the next. The sign offs will involve the steering committee, the 
project team and the users. 
Experienced consultants throughout the implementation will do regular quality checks. The ASAP 
implementation approach is primarily aimed at reducing risk and early realisation of business benefits.

The structure and membership of the project team emphasizes ownership, monitoring and control of the 
project through a hierarchy that reflects the structure of NOCMA and Twenty  Third Century Systems . 
Each level of control has well defined roles terms of reference and vertical links that facilitates 
communication and escalation of issues. 
 
Roles & Responsibilities: 
 
 
A key success factor for an ASAP Implementation is a strong core project team that consists of both  
NOCMA’s resources as well as Twenty Third Century Systems resources working hand -in-hand.  Specific 
roles include: 
 
Project Sponsor: 
The Project Sponsor will provide and communicate at large a vision of NOCMA’s long -term goals and 
objectives and general su pport of the project from a company level.  He/She will aid in resolution of 
issues that cannot be resolved within the team, and will ensure 24 hour turnaround on decision making. 
Time requirement averages 2 - 4 hours per week. The Project Sponsor: 
 
 Provides critical business guidance for the project team; 
 Makes resources available (budget); 
 Approves deliverables; 
 Determines disposition of critical high impact issues and change requests;  
 Ensures continued project support from executives; 
 Resolves strategic/policy issues, which cannot be satisfactorily resolved by the project team; and  
 Resolves resource conflicts, which impact the timing and quality of the project  
 
Project Steering Committee: 
This is the body that controls the whole project. It would be chai red by a member To Be Advised. It would 
be attended by the Project Sponsor, Project Directors, Project Managers, NOCMA’s selected Directors, 
and the Chief Auditor. The meetings would be held once a month. The steering committee:

Project Sponsor.  In general, NOCMA’s project manager will provide overall project management for the 
implementation as well as play an active role in the integration between the individual teams. The time 
requirement will average 20 to 25 hours per week.

NOCMA’s Organizational Change Management Responsibility: 
One essential responsibility for the consultant is to transf er the knowledge on how to effectively use the 
new system; the objective is to get the people ready. Effective Organizational Change Management 
(OCM) by NOCMA prepares the future users to accept the new system and adapt the operations 
accordingly. Without OCM, the consultants may spend more time “convincing” instead of “transferring 
knowledge”. Adopting the new solution means letting go of old work habits. Natural resistance can be 
minimized if some effort is given to managing the change. By ensuring that b enefits and consequences of 
the system change are well understood, OCM contributes to building commitment. This translates into a 
more rapid return on investment. The illustration below shows OCM throughout the project life.

NOCMA Organizational Change Management Responsibility 
One essential responsibility for the consultant is to transfer the knowledge on how to effectively use the 
new system; the objective is to get the people ready. Effective Organizational Change Management 
(OCM) by NOCMA prepares the future users to accept the new system and adapt the operations 
accordingly. Without OCM, the consultants may spend more time “convincing” instead of “transferring 
knowledge”. Adopting the new solution means letting go of old work habits. Natural resistance can be 
minimized if some (common-sense) effort is given to managing the change. By ensuring that benefits and 
consequences of the system change are well understood, OCM contributes to building commitment. This 
translates into a more rapid return on investment.

Scope Management 
The scope of the project will be defined in the blueprint. It is  a vital requirement that the project is 
restricted to this scope. Any development outside this agreed scope will threaten the project.  
As the project progresses, the necessity may arise to change the scope of the project. Any change will be 
governed by strict scope control procedures. These are intended to reduce the impact of the work on the 
project teams and the schedule. 
All scope change requests must be submitted to the project managers. They will investigate the change in 
terms of its impact on the project workload and schedule. If the change is outside the scope of the project, 
the costs will also be determined. Input from Twenty Third Century Systems product experts and NOCMA 
business people will be required to determine these costs and impacts. The  project managers will then 
present their findings to the project sponsor who will approve the change or reject it.  
 
Change Management 
At the heart of the project implementation success, Change Management is very crucial as it determines 
success of any project. 
Organizational Change Management, which encompasses the critical element of people readiness in a 
changing organization, is recognized as a key driver for successful project execution.  
It is generally accepted in information systems practice that p oor management of ‘soft’ or ‘people’ issues 
has been the reason for a large number of project failures. Issues here include managing stakeholder 
resistance, skills and competencies, and general organizational change readiness.  

=== PDF PAGE 136 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 136 of 275  
The objectives of the work to be performed are: 
 Reduce resistance to system and increase uptake in system usage.  
 Users have the required knowledge to use the system and access required information.  
 
Twenty Third Century Systems, will therefore propose that NOCMA, engages a change agent to ensure a 
successful project. 
 
Provision of Training 
Provision of training is to enable NOCMA staff to understand and use the application software. 
Twenty Third Century System undertakes to provide extensive training to super users during the proje ct 
implementation life -cycle. The super -users both functional and technical will receive hands -on training 
throughout the duration of the project as they will be working with TTCS consultants full -time on the 
project. This skills and knowledge transfer app roach “train the trainer” will give confidence, ownership and 
continued use of the new system by NOCMA with minimal fall back to TTCS or External assistance.  
 
TTCS will produce training manuals for all functional and technical for NOCMA system users. Thes e 
manuals will be NOCMA’s property for future use and reference. 
 
Goals of Super User Training 
Successful Super User training is one of the success criteria for the project. The goal of Super User 
training is to give Super User knowledge of the system and readiness to train End Users and use it in 
their own working area. 
 
For End -user training, it will be system based and will be conducted using the Quality Assurance 
Landscape that will be set -up for NOCMA.  NOCMA Super -users will train their end user count er parts 
with TTCS experienced consultants guidance. 
  
End-user training is not focused on SAP training solely.  It provides a blended learning path for the user 
that includes eLearning, system process, and business procedure training.  A proven methodolog y is 
employed which results in faster implementation and knowledge retention.  End -user training provides the 
right techniques to ensure that the training not only supports the solution but brings the end user 
community to a point of competency beyond go-live to ensure system optimisation & value addition. 
 
Description 
Twenty Third Century Systems has consultants who are experienced in the prepare me, tell me, show 
me, let me, help me training/education methodology. The consultants have conducted training o f SAP 
systems users for the over 45 implementations that the company has done. We have also been entrusted 
with training system users where implementations have been done by other SAP implementing partners 
other than ourselves. 
 
We understand that even though the SAP methodology provides for knowledge transfer throughout the 
project implementation, inevitably some gaps will arise due to a number of factors chief among them 
being limited acceptance of the system by the users. There  is also transition of members of staff in the 
productive phase of the system with new members of staff being engaged whilst others are moved up & 
across within the organization which gives rise to system knowledge gaps.  
 
NOCMA Value and Benefits 
End-user training helps NOCMA create, protect, and reap significant value from their SAP investment. It 
also enables the NOCMA to benefit from lower total cost of ownership and an ongoing, optimal return on 
investment throughout the project life cycle and beyond. NOCMA can reduce support costs with faster 
and higher acceptance among end users, achieve operational excellence and growth, and ensure faster 
adoption of new releases and changes in business processes. 
Upon delivery of End User Training NOCMA is e ncouraged to build a culture of continuous blended 
learning.  The delivery can help NOCMA design and implement learning strategies covering the entire 
corporate education value chain, offering: flexible curricula -- including standard and highly customized  
classes.

Approach, Methodologies and Deliverables 
Whilst we provide training based on the ASAP methodology for all the projects that we deploy for all our 
customers across Africa and abroad we also provide post SAP implementation end user training. The 
training we provide to end users is earmarked to provide system users with skills to enable them to 
achieve proficiency in utilizing the SAP system. Our understanding is that successful deployment & value 
addition of the SAP system is premised on the 3 pillars shown in the diagram below: 
 
 SAP AG, and  OTA Inc. / 5
Key Components of Implementations
Get the 
data 
ready
Get the 
people 
ready
Often the missing piece…
Get the 
system 
ready
 
 
TTCS understands that the three pillars form the basis of value beneficiation and are the pre -requisites 
for the successful deployment of the new SAP system. 
 
The Approach 
Our experience has proved that class room training based on PowerPoint presentations, supported with 
relevant visual aids provides the training participants with a sound conceptual understanding of the 
subject matter. However this has to be buttressed with  system based training combined with assessment 
tests to enable participants to acquire skills in utilization of the system.  
Ideally the number of users to be trained determines the strategy to be deployed in terms of how many 
trainers or the structuring of the classes in cases where there are limitations on the number of trainers 
available. Ordinarily the size of the class should be optimal for purposes of ensuring that the trainer can 
be able to provide one on one tell me, show me, let me & help me training. 
 
The Deliverables 
Our training fosters competency in deriving real value from deployed SAP applications. It is also provides 
a strong foundation for users to be able to effectively tackle SAP certification in their respective module 
areas to enable them to assume enriched responsibilities. 
We also deliver professionally prepared user manuals based specifically on the system deployed for the 
client. These provide users with reference material to use in their day to day functions as well as for the 
purposes of training any newly appointed members of staff. 
 
Interactive Teaching Programs as an Option 
TTCS will avail NOCMA with the Workforce Performance Builder, this functionality will be useful to 
NOCMA as a post implementation reference centre, can be use d continuously for training new personnel 
or re-training of staff.

The Workforce Performance Builder boosts operational efficiency by giving NOCMA workforce the SAP 
end-user training it needs, whenever employees need it.  The same tool can be used continu ously to 
easily create, edit, deploy, and track context -sensitive user help, transaction documentation, simulations, 
test scripts, and rapid e -learning materials with unmatched power and flexibility GWCL can put it to work 
almost immediately.    
 
With SAP Workforce Performance Builder, NOCMA can create user help as intuitively as recording a 
transaction. As NOCMA go through  a process, SAP Workforce Performance Builder can create a single -
source recording that NOCMA can distribute to its workforce i n whatever format that works best for 
GWCL’s.  For the fastest time to value, NOCMA can publish and use the recording almost immediately. 
Alternatively, NOCMAcan modify the output to include additional text, audio, rich media, or any degree of 
custom forma tting. Users also have the ability to interact with content authors as well  as the help desk 
and provide feedback on training materials and user help they receive.  
 
The cost of the Workforce Performance Builder will be dependent on the number of users.  
Data Migration Approach 
Data conversion is the process of transferring or copying data from an existing system to the productive 
system in SAP. Data from the third party systems operated by NOCMA including spread sheets for 
manually managed data will be adapted for upload in the SAP ERP system. 
 
The scope of the Data Conversion strategy basically will cover: 
 Data requirements: Data contents definition. 
 Data quality valuation: About reliability of managed data and information. 
 Data conversion stages: Proposi ng the schedule for the different tests to be done during 
implementation and customizing. 
 Improvements: Related to all the previous points, suggestions about how to increase data quality 
and improve its quality. 
 For NOCMA users, it's very important to clar ify that all the requirements established in this 
document must be covered providing the information in electronic format. 
 
Data Migration Aspects of the Cutover Plan 
A data migration plan will be developed which will detail the activities that need to be done from the 
following processes: 
 data management team mobilization by NOCMA 
 data management team training by TTCS 
 Submission of data collection templates to the data management team by TTCS  
 Commencement of data collection by NOCMA 
 Initial submission of data collected to module team leaders by NOCMA 
 Validation of submitted data by NOCMA/TTCS 
 Correction of any errors or gaps in the data by NOCMA 
 Resubmission of corrected data by NOCMA 
 Cleaning of the data by the NOCMA module team leaders  
 Signing off of the validated & verified data by the NOCMA project manager 
 Development of upload programs by the  TTCS consulting team 
 Testing of the upload programs by the consulting team 
 Submission of the data to consulting team for uploading by NOCMA 
 Uploading of data into the SAP  live system by NOCMA 
 Signing off of the uploaded data by the NOCMA  module team leaders and NOCMA project 
manager 
 
The data migration plan will then be incorporated into the cutover into the productive system plan. The 
cutover plan will include all the other activities that are required to check the readiness of the system for 
going live. A checklist will be deduced from the cutover plan & responsibilities assigned for management 
and control of the whole process to keep it on track. 
 
Important Information to note 

=== PDF PAGE 139 ===
 
Confidential                                                                                               NOCMA/NCB/S/ERP/2016/1  
   
        Page 139 of 275  
It is of paramount importance to take note that this data migration strategy is only indicative of how the 
process of extracting data from NOCMA’s legacy systems to the SAP system will be done. A much more 
precise document can only be produced at Business Blueprint phase when a study will be carried out on 
the nature & volumes of data NOCMA requires to be migrated. 
Data with anomalies will not be uploaded on the new system but will have to be re -worked to conform 
before being uploaded. For small volumes of data for instance less than 1000 entries, that data can be 
uploaded manually. This can be an opportunity for NOCMA’s personnel to learn the skill and knowledge 
of data migration manually.  
 
 
Conversion Methodology 
Strategy 
A five step process for the strategy is outlined below:  
 Identification of relevant fields  – in this step, the guidelines for the format of the data are 
determined, agreed upon and converted into excel sheet templates. The templates simplify the file 
layout for the data conversion. The template is made up of mandatory or optional fields, length of 
the fields and the type of data to be inputted for each data transfer object. This is the key step in 
the process and must be done with precision, ensuring tha t all stakeholders understand the 
guidelines. 
 
 Data Collection – the data will then be collected populating the excel sheet templates according 
to the format agreed in the identification of relevant fields.  
 
 Data Cleansing  – this is the process of verifying completeness & correctness of data. Upon 
completion of the verification, the data sheets will be converted into text files ready for upload. At 
this stage of the data conversion process, the number of items to be uploade d will also be 
evaluated in order to determine whether the data will be captured manually or automatically using 
upload programs. 
 
 Development of upload Programs  – in order to upload the data into the SAP system, programs 
have to be developed and tested. S amples of test data will be selected and testing will be carried 
out in the appropriate test environments of the SAP system. 
 
 Execution of the data transfer  – data transfer to the SAP system will be done through batch 
input programs. The programs will gene rate batches from the text files. By processing the batch, 
data will be updated into the SAP system automatically. The batches will be processed as 
background where the user can execute other functions in parallel on the screen or online with the 
option of correcting errors as they appear on the screen. 
 
 Verification of the uploaded data  – as a quality check on the upload process, there is need to 
check whether all the information was successfully uploaded into the SAP system. This will be 
done by generating related reports from the SAP system after completion of the data upload. Data 
on the system generated reports will be compared with the data on the spread sheets to check for 
completeness & correctness. 
 
Most of Data Conversion will have its origin in sy stems that NOCMA is presently operating. Other 
information could be obtained from excel files or other resources. This strategy provides for two main 
types of Data namely: 
 Master Data. 
 Transaction Data.

system in SAP. Data from the third party systems operated by NOCMA including spread sheets for 
manually managed data will be collated and converted in accordance with the data structures in SAP to
